<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For a signal with density bounded by <i>L</i> relative to uniform probability on $`S^{d-1}`$, we bound the information in a finite message <i>W</i> formed from exact Gaussian measurements, conditional on an independent projection revealed only to the analyst. For explicit row counts proportional to <i>d</i>, the bound is $`O(H(W)/d+d+\log(2+\log L))`$. Consequently, a finite-state learner with $`o(d^2)`$ persistent bits and a deterministic sample horizon needs $`\Omega(d\log(1/\epsilon))`$ fresh noiseless Gaussian measurements for constant-probability angular accuracy $`0\lt \epsilon\le1/10`$ under the uniform spherical prior.

- **claim-gaussian-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-gaussian-block)): For all sufficiently large $d$, set $$k=2\lfloor d/16\rfloor,\qquad m=k,\qquad t=m+1,\qquad \ell=4k.$$ Suppose $p=f\sigma_d$ is a probability with $0\le f\le L<\infty$. In the block experiment above, let $A$ have $k$ rows and let $B$ have $\ell$ rows. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\label{eq:gaussian-block} I(S;W\mid B,BS) \le \frac{H(W)}{t}+Cd+C\log(2+\log L).$$ The bound is uniform over the prior, the density bound $L$, and the message kernel. The labels used to produce $W$ are the exact values $AS$.
  - Explanation: After an independent projection is revealed to the analyst, the information a finite message carries about the signal is controlled by the message entropy divided by a number proportional to the dimension, plus a term linear in the dimension and a doubly logarithmic term in the density bound.
- **claim-streaming-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-streaming)): Let $M(d)=o(d^2)$ be nonnegative integer-valued, and let $0<\epsilon(d)\le1/10$. Suppose a family of learners in Definition <a href="#def:streaming-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:streaming-model">3</a> satisfies $$\mathbb P\{\arccos\langle\widehat S,S\rangle\le\epsilon(d)\}\ge\frac23 \qquad\text{when }S\sim\sigma_d,$$ where the probability includes the signal, samples, and learner randomness. Then there is an absolute $c>0$ such that $$T(d)\ge c\,d\log\frac1{\epsilon(d)}$$ for all sufficiently large $d$. The eventual threshold may depend on the memory sequence.
  - Explanation: A finite-state learner with subquadratic memory and a deterministic horizon needs a number of exact Gaussian observations proportional to the dimension times the log of inverse accuracy, under the uniform spherical prior.
- **claim-spherical-ball** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-spherical-ball)): Let $\sigma_q$ be uniform probability on $S^{q-1}$, where $q\ge2$. For every $z\in S^{q-1}$ and $r>0$, $$\label{eq:spherical-ball} \sigma_q\{u:\lVert u-z\rVert\le r\}\le r^{q-1}.$$ In particular an angular cap of radius $0<\epsilon\le\pi$ has mass at most $\epsilon^{q-1}$.
  - Explanation: A chordal ball on the uniform sphere has mass bounded by a power of its radius, and angular caps inherit the same bound.
- **claim-residual-sphere** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-residual-sphere)): Let $S\sim\sigma_d$ and let $G$ be independent of $S$, with $b$ rows, where $0\le b\le d-2$. For $b\ge1$, let $G$ be either a standard Gaussian matrix or a uniform Haar frame of $b$ orthonormal rows in $\mathbb R^d$. For $b=0$, interpret $G$ and $GS$ as empty. Conditional on $(G,GS)$, the signal is uniform on an affine sphere parallel to $\ker G$, with ambient kernel dimension $d-b$ and radius $$R=\lVert P_{\ker G}S\rVert.$$ For $b=0$, this is the original unit sphere and $R=1$ deterministically. For $1\le b\le d-2$, the squared radius has distribution $$\label{eq:residual-beta} R^2\sim\operatorname{Beta}\bigl((d-b)/2,b/2\bigr).$$ For $0<r_0<1$ one has $$\label{eq:residual-markov} \mathbb P\{R<r_0\}\le \frac{b}{d(1-r_0^2)}.$$ When $0\le b\le d/2$, two useful bounds at radius $1/2$ are $$\label{eq:residual-chebyshev} \mathbb P\{R<1/2\}\le\frac{8}{d+2}, \qquad \mathbb P\{R<1/2\}\le\frac{90}{d}.$$ The second inequality also follows directly from two Gaussian norm estimates, without using the beta variance.
  - Explanation: Conditioning a uniform signal on its exact measurements leaves a uniform point on an affine sphere; the squared radius follows a beta law and has explicit small-radius tail bounds.
- **claim-residual-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-residual-information)): Let $S\sim\sigma_d$, let $V$ be a random variable with values in a standard Borel space, jointly distributed with $S$, and let $G$ be independent of $(S,V)$, with $0\le b\le d-2$ rows. For $b\ge1$, $G$ may be either a standard Gaussian matrix or a uniform Haar frame of $b$ orthonormal rows; at $b=0$ it is empty. Let $\widehat S\in S^{d-1}$ be generated from $V$ by a kernel conditionally independent of $(S,G)$. Write $$p=\mathbb P\{\arccos\langle\widehat S,S\rangle\le\epsilon\}, \qquad R=\lVert P_{\ker G}S\rVert.$$ For $2\epsilon<r_0\le1$, if $\alpha:=p-\mathbb P\{R<r_0\}>0$, then $$\label{eq:residual-information} I(S;V\mid G,GS)\ge \alpha(d-b-1)\log\frac{r_0}{2\epsilon}-\log2.$$
  - Explanation: Accurate recovery inside the residual sphere forces conditional information growing linearly in the residual dimension times the log of the cap ratio, via a binary relative entropy comparison.
- **claim-endpoint-substitutions** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-replica-endpoints)): Assume $0<\epsilon\le1/10$ in Lemma <a href="#lem:residual-information" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:residual-information">6</a>. For sufficiently large $d$ the following bounds hold.* 1. *If $G$ is Gaussian, $p\ge3/5$, and $0\le b\le d/2$, the beta-variance bound (with the separate deterministic case at $b=0$) and the angular cap just described give $$\label{eq:endpoint-gaussian} I(S;V\mid G,GS)\ge \tfrac12(d-b-1)\log\frac1{5\epsilon}-\log2.$$* 2. *If $G$ is a Haar frame, $p\ge2/3$, and $b=\lfloor d/4\rfloor$, Markov’s bound $\mathbb P\{R<1/2\}\le4b/(3d)\le1/3$ gives $$\label{eq:endpoint-haar} I(S;V\mid G,GS)\ge \tfrac13(d-b-1)\log\frac1{4\epsilon}-\log2.$$* 3. *If $G$ is Gaussian, $p\ge3/5$, and $b=\lfloor d/2\rfloor$, the direct Gaussian bound $\mathbb P\{R<1/2\}\le90/d$ gives $$\label{eq:endpoint-bins} I(S;V\mid G,GS)\ge \tfrac12(d-b-1)\log\frac1{4\epsilon}-\log2.$$* *Each right side is at least $c\,d\log(1/\epsilon)$ for an absolute $c>0$ and all sufficiently large $d$.
  - Explanation: Three concrete choices of side projection give residual-information lower bounds, each of order dimension times log of inverse accuracy.
- **claim-borel-rules** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-borel-rules)): Fix $d\ge2$, a finite horizon $T$, and finite state sets. For fixed data-independent rule parameters, suppose each transition and stopping coordinate is measurable in the completion of the Borel sigma field on $\mathbb R^d\times\mathbb R$ under $\lambda(dx,dy)=\gamma_{1,d}(dx)\,dy$, where $\gamma_{1,d}$ is standard Gaussian probability on $\mathbb R^d$. The output law depends only on the terminal state and stopping index. There are Borel transition and stopping kernels, with the same state sets and output laws, having the same joint law of the signal, state path, stopping index, and output when $S\sim\sigma_d$.
  - Explanation: Completion-measurable transition and stopping rules can be replaced by Borel kernels without changing the joint law under the uniform prior.
- **claim-padding** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-padding)): Consider a learner in Definition <a href="#def:streaming-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:streaming-model">3</a> under the uniform prior. If its average success is at least $p$, some fixed shared-seed conditional experiment has success at least $p$. Its initialization remains independent of the signal and samples, and its fixed-seed Borel replacement has the same success. In this Borel experiment the initialization and all remaining randomized rules can be represented by data-independent variables chosen before sampling, followed by deterministic state-based rules. For every $p'<p$, some realization of those variables gives deterministic rules with average success at least $p'$.* *Padding a learner stopped by $T$ so that its halted states are absorbing, while retaining the stopping index and postponing the output, uses at most $(T+2)2^M$ states at every layer. Hence every padded state has entropy at most $$\label{eq:padded-entropy} h_T=M\log2+\log(T+2).$$ Extra ignored samples may be generated to complete a last block.
  - Explanation: Randomness can be fixed to give deterministic state-based rules, and stopping can be absorbed into a padded state space whose entropy is bounded by the memory plus a logarithm of the horizon.
- **claim-conditional-priors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-priors)): Let $S\sim\sigma_d$ and let $H$ be a finite-valued random variable jointly distributed with $S$. Every positive-probability value $h$ gives a conditional signal density bounded by $L_h=1/\mathbb P\{H=h\}$ relative to $\sigma_d$, and $$\label{eq:rare-event-entropy} \mathbb E\log L_H=H(H).$$
  - Explanation: Conditioning on a finite event gives a bounded density whose average log bound equals the entropy of the conditioning variable.
- **claim-early-clock** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-early-clock)): Consider a family of learners indexed by $d$, with uniform-prior success at least a fixed $p_0>0$, and set $\beta=\log(1/\epsilon)$ for $0<\epsilon\le1/10$. At each dimension, the terminal output capacity satisfies $$\label{eq:output-capacity} p_0\le (T+1)2^M\epsilon^{d-1}.$$ On the branch $T\le d\beta$, if $M=o(d^2)$, then $$\label{eq:early-clock} \beta=o(d),\qquad T=o(d^2),\qquad h_T=o(d^2).$$
  - Explanation: A capacity count on terminal outputs forces the accuracy parameter and horizon to be small compared with the dimension and its square on the short-horizon branch.
- **claim-raw-sample-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-raw-sample-bound)): Let $0<\epsilon\le1/10$, and let an estimator use all exact measurements $(G,GS)$ from $b\le\lfloor d/2\rfloor$ independent Gaussian rows, together with independent randomness, when $S\sim\sigma_d$. Its angular success probability is at most $$\label{eq:raw-sample-bound} \frac{8}{d+2}+(4\epsilon)^{d-b-1}.$$ The separate Gaussian-norm estimate gives the also valid bound $90/d+(4\epsilon)^{d-b-1}$. In particular, uniform-prior success at least $3/5$ requires $T>\lfloor d/2\rfloor$ for all sufficiently large $d$, even if all raw data up to the horizon are retained.
  - Explanation: Even retaining all raw exact data, fewer than about half the dimension of Gaussian rows cannot give constant-probability angular accuracy.
- **claim-replica-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-replica-information)): Let $t\ge1$ and suppose $K<\infty$. Let $W$ be finite-valued and suppose that every pair $(S_i,W)$ has the same law as $(S_1,W)$. In the conclusion write $S=S_1$. If $B$ is independent of $(S_1,\ldots,S_t,W)$, then $$\label{eq:replica-information} t I(S;W\mid B,\phi_B(S))\le H(W)+K-K',\qquad 0\le K'\le K.$$ The same statement holds in a conditional experiment when all of these hypotheses hold there, including the matching pair laws and the independence of $B$. Equivalently, the left side may use a separate representative of the common signal–message pair law if it is coupled independently with $B$.
  - Explanation: Message information after projection is bounded by message entropy plus the loss of total correlation among posterior replicas caused by the projection.
- **claim-projection-fibers** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-projection-fibers)): Let $1\le k\le d-2$ and let $P^0$ be either $\sigma_d$ or standard Gaussian probability on $\mathbb R^d$. Every full-row-rank $k\times d$ matrix $A$ projects $P^0$ to a Lebesgue density $p_A^0$. With $A^\dagger=A^{\mathsf T}(AA^{\mathsf T})^{-1}$, the spherical density is $$\label{eq:spherical-projection-density} p_A^0(y)=\frac{\Gamma(d/2)}{\pi^{k/2}\Gamma((d-k)/2)} \frac{(1-\lVert A^\dagger y\rVert^2)^{(d-k-2)/2}} {\sqrt{\det(AA^{\mathsf T})}} \mathbf 1_{\{\lVert A^\dagger y\rVert<1\}}.$$ For $\lVert A^\dagger y\rVert<1$, its conditional law $P^0_{A,y}$ is uniform on the sphere centered at $A^\dagger y$, of radius $\sqrt{1-\lVert A^\dagger y\rVert^2}$, in the affine space parallel to $\ker A$. For the Gaussian base, $p_A^0$ is the density of $N(0,AA^{\mathsf T})$, and the conditional point is $A^\dagger y$ plus a standard Gaussian in $\ker A$. These conditional laws have jointly measurable versions; unused fibers may be assigned any fixed probability law.* *If $m\ge1$, $t=m+1$, and $k+m\le d-2$, then $J>0$ almost surely under $(P^0)^{\otimes t}$ and $$\label{eq:inverse-affine-volume} \int J(\mathbf u)^{-k}\,(P^0)^{\otimes t}(d\mathbf u)<\infty.$$ If $k+m\le d-3$, the same assertion holds with exponent $k+1$.
  - Explanation: Gives the explicit projection density and fiber law for spherical and Gaussian bases, and finiteness of an inverse affine-volume moment.
- **claim-equal-label-measure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-equal-label-measure)): Let $k,m\ge1$, $t=m+1$, and $k+m\le d-2$. Let $P^0$ be either uniform probability on $S^{d-1}$ or standard Gaussian probability on $\mathbb R^d$, and let $\nu=fP^0$ for a bounded nonnegative Borel function $f$. With the notation in <a href="#eq:equal-label-geometry" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:equal-label-geometry">[eq:equal-label-geometry]</a> and <a href="#eq:finite-tilt" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-tilt">[eq:finite-tilt]</a>, for every nonnegative Borel function $H(A,y,\mathbf u)$, $$\begin{aligned} &\int\gamma_{k,d}(dA)\int_{\mathbb R^k}p_A(y)^t \int H(A,y,\mathbf u)\,\nu_{A,y}^{\otimes t}(d\mathbf u)\,dy \notag\\ &\quad=(2\pi)^{-km/2}\int J(\mathbf u)^{-k}\,\nu^{\otimes t}(d\mathbf u) \int H(A,Au_1,\mathbf u)\,\gamma_{k,d}^{F_{\mathbf u}}(dA). \label{eq:equal-label-measure} \end{aligned}$$ Both sides are finite measures before the test $H$ is inserted. They are supported on $Au_i=y$ for every $i$, full-column-rank $D_{\mathbf u}$, and full-row-rank $A$. In the spherical case the common fiber has positive radius. Thus <a href="#eq:equal-label-measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:equal-label-measure">[eq:equal-label-measure]</a> is an identity of finite measures on $(A,y,\mathbf u)$; the right side places $y$ at $Au_1$.* *For a full-rank $A$, alternatively define $p_A(y)$ by the lower limit of its radius-$1/n$ Lebesgue ball averages or by the lower limit of its Gaussian convolutions with covariance $n^{-2}I_k$. Each choice is jointly measurable and agrees with <a href="#eq:finite-tilt" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-tilt">[eq:finite-tilt]</a> for Lebesgue-almost every $y$. All three versions agree at $y=Au_1$, with a finite positive value, almost everywhere for the finite reference measure $$\label{eq:equal-label-reference} R(d\mathbf u,dA)=\nu^{\otimes t}(d\mathbf u) \gamma_{k,d}^{F_{\mathbf u}}(dA).$$ When $\nu=0$ the last assertion is vacuous. The equality is obtained using either ball or Gaussian approximate identities in both integration orders for the base law $P^0$, then multiplying the resulting measure identity by the bounded density factors.
  - Explanation: Identifies exactly two finite measures on equal-label tuples: one by fibers over a common label, one by independent points weighted by inverse affine volume.
- **claim-replica-likelihood** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-replica-likelihood)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:equal-label-measure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:equal-label-measure">15</a>, suppose $p=fP^0$ is a probability. Draw $A\sim\gamma_{k,d}$ independently of $S\sim p$, put $Y=AS$, and conditionally on $(A,Y)$ draw $t$ independent replicas from $p_{A,Y}$. Let $Q$ be the law of their tuple together with $A$, and let $R=p^{\otimes t}(d\mathbf u)\gamma_{k,d}^{F_{\mathbf u}}(dA)$. Then $R$ is a probability and $$\label{eq:replica-likelihood} \frac{dQ}{dR}(\mathbf u,A) =(2\pi)^{-km/2}J(\mathbf u)^{-k}p_A(Au_1)^{-m}.$$ In particular, the tuple marginal of $Q$ is absolutely continuous relative to $p^{\otimes t}$.
  - Explanation: Gives the density of the posterior-replica law relative to an explicit probability reference, showing the tuple law is absolutely continuous to the product prior.
- **claim-replica-logs** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-replica-logs)): In Corollary <a href="#cor:replica-likelihood" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:replica-likelihood">16</a>, let $p=f\sigma_d$ with $0\le f\le L<\infty$. Under the replica law $Q$, both $\log p_A(Y)$ and $\log J(S_1,\ldots,S_t)$ are absolutely integrable. Consequently the tuple total correlation is finite and satisfies $$\label{eq:replica-correlation-upper} K\le -\frac{km}{2}\log(2\pi)-k\mathbb E_Q\log J-m\mathbb E_Q\log p_A(Y).$$
  - Explanation: For bounded spherical priors the relevant logarithms are integrable, so the replica total correlation is finite and bounded above.
- **claim-gaussian-test** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-test)): Let $G$ be an $\ell\times m$ standard Gaussian matrix and set $$Z_{k,m}=\mathbb E\det(G^{\mathsf T}G)^{-k/2}.$$ This constant is finite and positive. The nonnegative function $$\label{eq:gaussian-projected-test} \Lambda(B,U_1,\ldots,U_t) =\frac{c}{Z_{k,m}} \det((BD)^{\mathsf T}(BD))^{-k/2} p_{C_0B}(C_0U_1)^{-m},$$ with value zero on the exceptional null sets, has expectation one under the law that draws $B$ and projects independent $p$-distributed signals. Conditional on the replica tuple and its original matrix $A$, $\widetilde A$ has law $\gamma_{k,d}^{F}$; this law depends only on the tuple. If $K'$ is the projected total correlation of the replicas, then $$\label{eq:gaussian-correlation-loss} K-K'\le Ckm+ m\mathbb E\!\left[\log p_{\widetilde A}(\widetilde A S_1)-\log p_A(Y)\right].$$ Every logarithm in this display is absolutely integrable.
  - Explanation: An observable likelihood test built from the projected replicas has expectation one and removes the affine-volume term, bounding the correlation loss.
- **claim-gaussian-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-density)): In the replica experiment with the independent side matrix and $\widetilde A=C_0B$ defined above, $$\label{eq:gaussian-density-comparison} \mathbb E\!\left[\log p_{\widetilde A}(\widetilde A S_1)-\log p_A(Y)\right] \le Ck+C\log(2+\log L).$$
  - Explanation: A dyadic decomposition bounds the expected log ratio of the two projection densities by a term linear in the row count plus a doubly logarithmic term.
- **claim-distance-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-distance-block)): Let $P=f\sigma_d$ be a probability with a nonnegative Borel density $f\le e^b$, where $b\ge0$. Let $S\sim P$, let $A$ be an independent $k\times d$ standard Gaussian matrix, and put $Y=AS$. Let a finite message $W$ be drawn from a kernel of $(A,Y)$, conditionally independently of $S$. Let $B$ be a $p\times d$ standard Gaussian matrix independent of the entire joint experiment producing $(S,A,Y,W)$. Then $$\label{eq:distance-block} I(S;W\mid B,BS)\le\frac{H(W)}q+C\left(d+\frac bd\right)$$ for an absolute $C$, uniformly over the prior and message kernel.
  - Explanation: A distance-bin test gives a block information bound whose prior-density cost is linear in the log density bound divided by the dimension.
- **claim-distance-heights** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-distance-heights)): The heights are positive almost surely and obey $$\label{eq:distance-height} \rho_i\le2,\qquad \mathbb E\log^-\rho_i\le C\left(1+\frac bd\right), \qquad \log^-x:=\max\{0,-\log x\}.$$
  - Explanation: Simplex heights are positive and at most two, with expected negative logarithm controlled by a constant plus the density budget over the dimension.
- **claim-gaussian-streaming** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-gaussian-streaming)): Let $M(d)=o(d^2)$ be nonnegative and integer-valued, and let $0<\epsilon(d)\le1/10$. A learner in Definition <a href="#def:streaming-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:streaming-model">3</a> whose uniform-prior angular success is at least $3/5$ satisfies $$T(d)\ge c\,d\log(1/\epsilon(d))$$ for an absolute $c>0$ and all sufficiently large $d$. The threshold may depend on the memory sequence.
  - Explanation: Iterating the Gaussian block bound over current padded states gives the sample horizon lower bound for learners with constant-probability success.
- **claim-distance-streaming** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-distance-streaming)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:streaming" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:streaming">2</a>, the distance-bin block estimate <a href="#eq:distance-block" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:distance-block">[eq:distance-block]</a> also gives $T(d)\ge c\,d\log(1/\epsilon(d))$ for an absolute $c>0$ and all sufficiently large $d$.
  - Explanation: The distance-bin block estimate yields the same horizon lower bound by a second current-state iteration.
- **claim-haar-coarea** (theorem, [source](#stmt-haar-coarea)): For the dimensions in <a href="#haar:dimensions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="haar:dimensions">[haar:dimensions]</a> and every probability $P=g\sigma$ with $0\le g\le K<\infty$, the following finite measures on the frame and the whole tuple are equal: $$\label{haar:coarea-identity} \mathrm dA\int_{\mathbb R^k}p_A(y)^m\,\mathrm dy \prod_{i=0}^r P_{A,y}(\mathrm ds_i) =\left(\prod_{i=0}^rP(\mathrm ds_i)\right) \left(\prod_{i=1}^r\alpha_{d-i+1,k}\delta_i^{-k}\right) \mathrm dA_{D_r}.$$ Equality means equality after integration against every bounded Borel function of $(A,\boldsymbol s)$. Define the displayed product density to be zero on degenerate tuples. The measure on the right is supported on tuples with $\dim D_i=i$ for all $1\le i\le r$.
  - Explanation: Equates the posterior multi-replica measure integrated over a Haar frame with a product of priors weighted by successive inverse simplex heights.
- **claim-haar-upper-correlation** (bound, [source](#stmt-haar-upper-correlation)): The correlations satisfy $0\le J_Z\le J_S\le U<\infty$, where $$\begin{aligned} U&=\sum_{i=1}^r\left[ \log\alpha_{d-i+1,k}+k\mathbb E_Q\log(1/\delta_i) -\mathbb E_{A,S\sim P}\log p_A(AS)\right], \label{haar:upper-U}\\ 0\le U&\le Crd(\log K+\log d).\label{haar:crude-U} \end{aligned}$$ Here $\mathbb E_{A,S\sim P}$ draws the Haar frame independently of a vector with law $P$. Every logarithm in <a href="#haar:upper-U" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="haar:upper-U">[haar:upper-U]</a> is integrable.
  - Explanation: Provides a finite explicit upper bound on the unprojected replica correlation, ordered above the projected one.
- **claim-haar-maximal** (bound, [source](#stmt-haar-maximal)): Suppose $f$ is a probability density supported in the unit ball of $\mathbb R^k$ and $f\le K\alpha_{d,k}$. Then $$\label{haar:maximal-log} \int f(u)\log\frac{\mathcal M f(u)}{f(u)}\,\mathrm du \le Cd+\log(1+Cd+\log K).$$ Both logarithms are integrable under $f(u)\mathrm du$. The integrand is taken as zero on $\{f=0\}$.
  - Explanation: A centered maximal average controls the log ratio of a bounded density to its maximal function through a weak-type volume estimate.
- **claim-haar-lower-correlation** (bound, [source](#stmt-haar-lower-correlation)): For the replica law above and every $0<a<1$, $$\label{haar:lower-Z} J_Z\ge a\sum_{i=1}^r\left[ k\mathbb E_Q\log(1/\delta_i)-\log(C_1^kv_k) -\mathbb E_{A,S\sim P}\log\mathcal M p_A(AS)\right] +r\log(1-a),$$ where $C_1$ is an absolute constant, independent of $P,K,d,a$.
  - Explanation: A distribution-function test on projected distances lower-bounds the projected correlation, valid even with atoms.
- **claim-haar-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-haar-block)): Let $S\sim\sigma$, let $V$ be a finite-valued state, and let a block consist of $k=\lfloor d/16\rfloor$ fresh independent standard Gaussian designs, independent of $(S,V)$ and the past, with their exact labels. Suppose a finite-valued state $W$ is generated by a measurable randomized kernel from $V$ and the full block, with no other retained information from the past. Let $B$ be a Haar $\ell=\lfloor d/4\rfloor$ frame drawn independently of the entire joint experiment producing $S,V$, the block, and $W$. Then $$\begin{aligned} I(S;W\mid V,B,BS)\le{}&\frac{H(W\mid V)}{m}+Cd +\log(1+Cd+H(V))\notag\\ &+C\frac{H(V)+\log d}{d^2}, \qquad m=\lfloor d/16\rfloor+1. \label{haar:state-budget} \end{aligned}$$ The constant $C$ is absolute. If every state has logarithmic width at most $H_*=o(d^2)$, the left side is at most $Cd$ for all sufficiently large $d$.
  - Explanation: Cancelling most of the height terms gives a block information bound that is linear in the dimension when state entropies are subquadratic.
- **claim-haar-terminal** (bound, [source](#stmt-haar-terminal)): Let $S\sim\sigma$, let $V_f$ be finite-valued, and let the unit-valued output $\widehat S$ be generated from $V_f$ by a measurable kernel using fresh randomness, conditionally independently of $S$. Let $B$ be a Haar $\ell=\lfloor d/4\rfloor$ frame independent of $(S,V_f,\widehat S)$. If $\mathbb P\{\arccos\langle\widehat S,S\rangle\le\epsilon\}\ge2/3$ for $0<\epsilon\le1/10$, then $$\label{haar:terminal-information} I(S;V_f\mid B,BS) \ge\frac{d-\ell-1}{3}\log\frac1{4\epsilon}-\log2.$$
  - Explanation: Constant-probability accurate output forces terminal information on the Haar residual fiber of order dimension times log inverse accuracy.
- **claim-haar-streaming** (bound, [source](#stmt-haar-streaming)): For each dimension $d$, let $S\sim\sigma$ and consider a learner in Definition <a href="#def:streaming-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:streaming-model">3</a>, with deterministic horizon $T=T(d)$ and nonnegative integer memory $M=M(d)$. If $M(d)=o(d^2)$, $0<\epsilon(d)\le1/10$, and its angular success probability averaged over the signal, samples, and learner randomness is at least $2/3$, then $$T(d)=\Omega\bigl(d\log(1/\epsilon(d))\bigr).$$ The implicit constant is absolute; the dimension threshold may depend on the memory sequence.
  - Explanation: The Haar current-state argument gives the sample horizon lower bound for finite-state learners.
- **claim-synthetic-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-syn-block)): Let $P=f\sigma_d$ be a probability, where $f$ is a nonnegative Borel function satisfying $f\le\Lambda<\infty$, with $\Lambda\ge1$. Draw $X\sim P$ and $A\sim\gamma_{k,d}$ independently. Let $z=z(A,AX)$ be a measurable function taking at most $K\ge1$ values. Independently of this entire experiment draw $B\sim\gamma_{m,d}$, and put $Y=BX$. With the parameters in <a href="#syn:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="syn:parameters">[syn:parameters]</a>, $$\label{syn:block-bound} I(X;z\mid B,Y) \le \frac{\log K}{\ell} +C\bigl[d+\log(1+\log\Lambda)\bigr],$$ where $C$ is absolute and independent of $P,\Lambda,K$, and the message rule.
  - Explanation: Using synthetic Gaussian rows in the complement of the projected differences bounds the information beyond an independent Gaussian projection.
- **claim-synthetic-kernel** (bound, [source](#stmt-syn-synthetic)): Let $P=f\sigma_d$ be as in Proposition <a href="#syn:block" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="syn:block">32</a>. Fix a tuple $\mathbf x\in(S^{d-1})^\ell$ of affine rank $u$, and put $$x=x_1,\qquad L=L_{\mathbf x},\qquad \delta(s)=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(s-x,L).$$ Draw $B\sim\gamma_{m,d}$. Given $B$, draw $F\sim\gamma_{k,m}^{BL}$, so each row of $F$ is a standard Gaussian in $(BL)^\perp$. Then $$\label{syn:synthetic-bound} \mathbb E_{B,F}g_{FB}(FBx) \le (2\pi)^{-k/2}a_q\int\delta(s)^{-k}\,P(ds),\qquad a_q=\mathbb E\det(HH^{\mathsf T})^{-1/2}\le(q-k-1)^{-k/2},$$ where $H$ is a standard Gaussian $k\times q$ matrix.* *There is an absolute $C_0>0$ such that the following holds with $$\label{syn:cutoff} r_*=\exp\!\left[-\frac{C_0(d+\log\Lambda)}{d-1-u-k}\right]\in(0,1).$$ For every full-row-rank $k\times d$ matrix $A$, put $a=\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}$ and define $$\begin{aligned} U_A(b)={}&(2\pi)^{-k/2}\mathbf 1_{\{\lVert b\rVert\le a\}} \left[1+a^k\int g_A(v)\mathbf 1_{\{\lVert v-b\rVert\le2a\}}\right. \notag\\[-2mm] &\hspace{42mm}\left. {}\cdot\min\{(ar_*)^{-k},\lVert v-b\rVert^{-k}\}\,dv\right]. \label{syn:majorant} \end{aligned}$$ At $v=b$, the second entry in the minimum is interpreted as $+\infty$. If $Ax=b$ and $L\subset\ker A$, then $$\label{syn:kernel-bound} (2\pi)^{-k/2}\int\delta(s)^{-k}\,P(ds)\le U_A(b).$$ Writing $V_k$ for the volume of the Euclidean unit ball in $\mathbb R^k$, the same function satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \int U_A(b)\,db &\le (2\pi)^{-k/2}V_k a^k[2+k\log(2/r_*)], \label{syn:majorant-mass}\\ \mathbb E_{A\sim\gamma_{k,d}}\log\int U_A(b)\,db &\le C\bigl[d+\log(1+\log\Lambda)\bigr]. \label{syn:majorant-log} \end{aligned}$$ The logarithm in the last display is absolutely integrable, and $\mathbb E_A\log(1+\int U_A)<\infty$.
  - Explanation: Controls the synthetic density by an inverse-distance kernel and a majorant whose mass and log expectation are bounded.
- **claim-synthetic-logs** (bound, [source](#stmt-syn-logs)): In the replica experiment $Q$, enlarged by the independent $B$ and the conditional $F$ above, $$\mathbb E\|\log w(\mathbf Y)\|+\mathbb E\|\log\Xi\| +\mathbb E\|\log R_B(\mathbf Y)\|<\infty.$$ If $G$ is a standard Gaussian $m\times u$ matrix, then $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E\log w(\mathbf Y) &=\mathbb E\log w(\mathbf X)-\frac{k}{2}\mathbb E\log\det(G^{\mathsf T}G), \label{syn:gram-log}\\ \frac12\mathbb E\log\det(G^{\mathsf T}G)&\le\frac u2\log m. \label{syn:gram-upper} \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $$\label{syn:correlation-lower} \mathcal C_Y \ge\mathbb E\log R_B(\mathbf Y) \ge\mathbb E\log w(\mathbf Y)-u\,\mathbb E\log\Xi.$$
  - Explanation: Establishes integrability of the projected comparison logarithms and a lower bound on the projected correlation.
- **claim-synthetic-streaming** (bound, [source](#stmt-syn-streaming)): For every nonnegative integer-valued $M(d)=o(d^2)$ and every sequence $0<\epsilon(d)\le1/10$, consider learners in Definition <a href="#def:streaming-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:streaming-model">3</a> with deterministic horizon $T(d)$ and signal $X\sim\sigma_d$. If their angular success probability, averaged over the uniform signal, rows, and learner randomness, is at least $2/3$, then $$T(d)\ge c\,d\log(1/\epsilon(d))$$ for all sufficiently large $d$, with an absolute $c>0$. The threshold may depend on the memory sequence.
  - Explanation: Iterating the synthetic block bound along full state histories yields the sample horizon lower bound.
- **claim-incidence-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-block)): Let $\mu=f\sigma$ be a probability measure with a Borel density $0\le f\le F<\infty$, where $F\ge1$. Draw $S\sim\mu$ and standard Gaussian matrices $A\in\mathbb R^{n\times d}$ and $B\in\mathbb R^{p\times d}$, all independently. For an integer $w\ge1$, a message $J$ takes values in a fixed $w$-point alphabet (unused symbols are allowed) and is drawn by an arbitrary measurable probability kernel from $(A,AS)$; conditional on $(A,AS)$, its randomness is independent of $(S,B)$. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\label{inc:block-bound} I_\mu(S;J\mid B,BS) \le \frac{\log w}{k}+Cd+C\log(1+\log F).$$
  - Explanation: Single-block information is bounded by message log-size over the row count plus linear and doubly logarithmic terms.
- **claim-incidence-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inc-product)): Put $Z_i=Bs_i$. After $A,C$ are discarded under $R$, $$\label{inc:product-law} \mathcal L_R(\mathbf s\mid B,Z_1,\ldots,Z_k,J) =\bigotimes_{i=1}^k\mu_{B,Z_i}.$$ Consequently, $$\label{inc:entropy-lower} \operatorname{KL}(Q\\|R)\ge kI_\mu(S;J\mid B,BS).$$
  - Explanation: Under the second law the signals are conditionally independent given their side projections, so the divergence dominates a multiple of the block information.
- **claim-incidence-projection** (classification, [source](#stmt-inc-projection)): Suppose $L\in\mathbb R^{q\times d}$ has full row rank and $1\le q\le d-2$. In the interior $$E_L=\{t:t^{\mathsf T}(LL^{\mathsf T})^{-1}t<1\}$$ define a finite measure on $\{s\in\mathbb S^{d-1}:Ls=t\}$ by $$\label{inc:fiber-measure} \lambda_{L,t}(ds)= \frac{f(s)}{\|\mathbb S^{d-1}\|\,j_L(s)} \,d\operatorname{vol}_{d-1-q}(s),\qquad j_L(s)=\det\!\bigl(L(I-ss^{\mathsf T})L^{\mathsf T}\bigr)^{1/2}.$$ Set $\lambda_{L,t}=0$ outside $E_L$. Its mass and normalization are exactly the versions recalled in <a href="#inc:recalled-fibers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inc:recalled-fibers">[inc:recalled-fibers]</a>: $$p_L(t)=\lambda_{L,t}(\mathbb S^{d-1}),\qquad \mu_{L,t}=\lambda_{L,t}/p_L(t)\quad\text{where }p_L(t)>0.$$ The finite-measure kernel is jointly measurable in $(L,t)$. Moreover, $$\label{inc:ellipsoid-bound} V_L\\|p_L\\|_\infty\le Fd^d,\qquad V_L=\|E_L\|=v_q\det(LL^{\mathsf T})^{1/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Defines explicit fiber measures with normal Jacobian and bounds the ellipsoid volume times the sup of the projection density.
- **claim-incidence-swap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inc-swap)): Let $X,Z$ be second-countable Riemannian manifolds, and let $G:X\times Z\to\mathbb R^a$ be smooth. On a relatively open subset $\Omega$ of $G^{-1}(0)$, suppose both partial differentials $d_xG$ and $d_zG$ are onto. Write $J_xG,J_zG$ for their normal Jacobians. For every nonnegative measurable $h$ on $\Omega$, $$\begin{aligned} &\int_Z\int_{\{x:(x,z)\in\Omega\}} \frac{h(x,z)}{J_xG(x,z)} \,d\operatorname{vol}_{\dim X-a}(x)\,d\operatorname{vol}_Z(z) \notag\\ &\qquad= \int_X\int_{\{z:(x,z)\in\Omega\}} \frac{h(x,z)}{J_zG(x,z)} \,d\operatorname{vol}_{\dim Z-a}(z)\,d\operatorname{vol}_X(x). \label{inc:swap-identity} \end{aligned}$$ The fiber volumes are induced volumes, and the equality holds for nonnegative integrals even when their common value is infinite.
  - Explanation: Allows interchange of integration order on a regular incidence set using normal Jacobians.
- **claim-incidence-densities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inc-densities)): Define the following marginal probability measures of $\mathsf H$: $$\begin{split} \mathsf H_A(d\mathbf s,dA) &=\mu^{\otimes k}(d\mathbf s)\,\gamma_U(dA),\\ \mathsf H_C(d\mathbf s,dB,dC) &=\mu^{\otimes k}(d\mathbf s)\,\gamma_{p,d}(dB)\,\upsilon_{BU}(dC). \end{split}$$ Using the unnormalized measures of <a href="#inc:fiber-measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inc:fiber-measure">[inc:fiber-measure]</a>, define nonnegative measures on the corresponding exact incidence sets by $$\begin{aligned} \mathsf M_A(d\mathbf s,dA) &=\mu(ds_1)\gamma_{n,d}(dA) \prod_{i=2}^k\lambda_{A,As_1}(ds_i),\label{inc:MA}\\ \mathsf M_C(d\mathbf s,dB,dC) &=\mu(ds_1)\gamma_{p,d}(dB)\upsilon_{n,p}(dC) \prod_{i=2}^k\lambda_{D,Ds_1}(ds_i),\qquad D=CB.\label{inc:MC} \end{aligned}$$ Values at row-rank failures may be set to zero. The weights on the right below are assigned arbitrary finite values on their Gram-rank null sets. These measures satisfy the exact identities $$\begin{aligned} \mathsf M_A &=(2\pi)^{-n\ell/2}\det(U^{\mathsf T}U)^{-n/2}\,\mathsf H_A, \label{inc:undivided-A}\\ \mathsf M_C &=\kappa\det(U^{\mathsf T}B^{\mathsf T}BU)^{-n/2}\,\mathsf H_C, \qquad \kappa=\frac{\|\mathcal V_{n,p-\ell}\|}{\|\mathcal V_{n,p}\|}. \label{inc:undivided-C} \end{aligned}$$ These are equalities of nonnegative measures.* *For $L=A,D$, the values $p_L(Ls_1)$ are positive and finite $\mathsf H$-almost surely and equal the shrinking-ball limits $$p_L(Ls_1)=\lim_{j\to\infty} \frac{\mu\{z:\\|Lz-Ls_1\\|<2^{-j}\}}{v_n2^{-jn}}.$$ On this full-measure set, the densities of $Q$ and $R$ with respect to $\mathsf H$ are $$\begin{aligned} q_*&=w\phi_J(A,As_1)(2\pi)^{-n\ell/2} \det(U^{\mathsf T}U)^{-n/2}p_A(As_1)^{-\ell}, \label{inc:q-density}\\ r_*&=\kappa\det(U^{\mathsf T}B^{\mathsf T}BU)^{-n/2} p_D(Ds_1)^{-\ell}.\label{inc:r-density} \end{aligned}$$ In particular $Q\ll R$.
  - Explanation: Gives exact undivided measure identities and the densities of the two laws relative to a common law, proving absolute continuity.
- **claim-stiefel-volume** (classification, [source](#stmt-inc-stiefel)): For $1\le n\le h$, with the induced Euclidean matrix metric, $$\label{inc:stiefel-volume} \|\mathcal V_{n,h}\|= 2^{n(n-1)/4}\prod_{j=1}^n\|\mathbb S^{h-j}\|.$$ Consequently, for <a href="#inc:dimensions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inc:dimensions">[inc:dimensions]</a>, $$\label{inc:kappa} \kappa=\pi^{-n\ell/2}\prod_{j=1}^n \frac{\Gamma((p-j+1)/2)}{\Gamma((p-j+1-\ell)/2)} \ge\left(\frac{p-n+1-\ell}{2\pi}\right)^{n\ell/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Computes the induced Stiefel volume and a lower bound for the volume ratio used in the likelihood.
- **claim-incidence-kernel** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-kernel)): Under $Q$, define $$\begin{split} K&=\int \operatorname{dist}(z-s_1,\operatorname{col}U)^{-n}\,d\mu(z),\\ p_A^*(t)&=\sup_{\rho>0} \frac{\mu\{z:\\|Az-t\\|<\rho\}}{v_n\rho^n},\qquad K_0=v_n\\|A\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^n p_A^*(As_1). \end{split}$$ Then $K_0\ge2^{-n}$, and $$\label{inc:kernel-bound} K\le K_0L_F,\qquad L_F=1+\frac{n}{d-\ell-n}+n\log(2/\tau),\qquad \tau=\left(\frac{2^{-n}}{Fd^d}\right)^{1/(d-\ell-n)}.$$ In particular, $\log L_F\le C\log d+C\log(1+\log F)$, and $K<\infty$. Moreover, $$\label{inc:conditional-density} \mathbb E_Q[p_D(Ds_1)\mid\mathbf s,A,y,J] \le (2\pi)^{-n/2}K .$$
  - Explanation: Bounds an inverse-distance kernel at two scales by an operator-norm term and a codimension term.
- **claim-incidence-maximal** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-maximal)): For every full-rank $A$, $$\label{inc:maximal-bound} \int\log\frac{p_A^*(As)}{p_A(As)}\,d\mu(s) \le Cd+C\log(1+\log F).$$ The integrand is nonnegative almost surely.
  - Explanation: Averaged maximal-to-actual density log ratio at actual labels is bounded linearly in the dimension plus a doubly logarithmic term.
- **claim-incidence-precision** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-precision)): There is a universal $c>0$ with the following property. Let $M=M(d)$ be a nonnegative integer with $M(d)=o(d^2)$. For all sufficiently large $d$, with the threshold allowed to depend on the rate $M(d)/d^2\to0$, suppose $S\sim\sigma$ and a learner in Definition <a href="#def:streaming-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:streaming-model">3</a> stops by a deterministic integer horizon $T\ge0$. If $$\Pr\{\angle(\widehat S,S)\le\epsilon\}\ge2/3, \qquad 0<\epsilon\le1/10,$$ then $$\label{inc:precision-bound} T\ge c\,d\log(1/\epsilon).$$ The bound permits arbitrary computation within a transition.
  - Explanation: The incidence route gives the average-prior horizon lower bound for finite-state learners.
- **claim-incidence-short** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-short)): Put $L_\epsilon=\log(1/\epsilon)$. Pad stopping to $T$ by retaining the stopped state and its stopping index. At most $$\label{inc:width} w=(T+2)2^M$$ padded states suffice. Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#inc:precision" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inc:precision">44</a>, if $T<dL_\epsilon$, then $$\label{inc:short-bounds} L_\epsilon=O(1+M/d)=o(d),\qquad T=O(d^2),\qquad \log w=o(d^2).$$
  - Explanation: In the short-horizon case padded state count is bounded and precision, horizon and log-width are small relative to dimension scales.
- **claim-incidence-history** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-history)): In the short-horizon case of Lemma <a href="#inc:short" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inc:short">45</a>, put $b=\lceil T/n\rceil$ and pad with fresh unused samples to $bn$ observations. Let $V_i$ be the transcript of the first $i$ block-end padded states, with $V_0$ trivial. Let $B\sim\gamma_{p,d}$ be independent of the entire learner experiment and used only for analysis. Then, for all sufficiently large $d$, $$\label{inc:history-bound} I(S;V_b\mid B,BS)\le Cbd$$ for an absolute $C$. The dimension threshold may depend on the rate $M/d^2\to0$.
  - Explanation: Information about the signal accumulated over block histories grows at most linearly in the number of blocks times the dimension.
- **claim-incidence-fiber-precision** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-fiber-precision)): Let $S\sim\sigma$, and let a unit-vector output $\widehat S$ have angular error at most $\epsilon\le1/10$ with probability at least $2/3$. Let $B\sim\gamma_{p,d}$, $p=\lfloor d/4\rfloor$, be independent of the entire experiment producing $(S,\widehat S)$. Then, for all sufficiently large $d$, $$\label{inc:fiber-bound} I(S;\widehat S\mid B,BS) \ge \frac{d-p-1}{3}\log\frac1{4\epsilon}-\log2 \ge c_1dL_\epsilon$$ with an absolute $c_1>0$. In the learner model above, success probability at least $2/3$ is also impossible for $T\le d/4$, even if all observations are retained.
  - Explanation: Accurate output inside an independent fiber needs information of order dimension times log inverse accuracy, and success is impossible for short horizons even with full retention.

For $b\\ge1$, $G$ may be either a standard Gaussian matrix or a uniform Haar frame of $b$ orthonormal rows; at $b=0$ it is empty. Let $\\widehat S\\in S^{d-1}$ be generated from $V$ by a kernel conditionally independent of $(S,G)$. Write $$p=\\mathbb P\\{\\arccos\\langle\\widehat S,S\\rangle\\le\\epsilon\\},\n \\qquad R=\\lVert P_{\\ker G}S\\rVert.$$ For $2\\epsilon<r_0\\le1$, if $\\alpha:=p-\\mathbb P\\{R<r_0\\}>0$, then $$\\label{eq:residual-information}\n I(S;V\\mid G,GS)\\ge\n \\alpha(d-b-1)\\log\\frac{r_0}{2\\epsilon}-\\log2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma_d$, let $V$ be a random variable with values in a standard Borel space, jointly distributed with $S$, and let $G$ be independent of $(S,V)$, with $0\\le b\\le d-2$ rows. For $b\\ge1$, $G$ may be either a standard Gaussian matrix or a uniform Haar frame of $b$ orthonormal rows; at $b=0$ it is empty. Let $\\widehat S\\in S^{d-1}$ be generated from $V$ by a kernel conditionally independent of $(S,G)$. Write $$p=\\mathbb P\\{\\arccos\\langle\\widehat S,S\\rangle\\le\\epsilon\\},\n \\qquad R=\\lVert P_{\\ker G}S\\rVert.$$ For $2\\epsilon<r_0\\le1$, if $\\alpha:=p-\\mathbb P\\{R<r_0\\}>0$, then $$\\label{eq:residual-information}\n I(S;V\\mid G,GS)\\ge\n \\alpha(d-b-1)\\log\\frac{r_0}{2\\epsilon}-\\log2.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-replica-endpoints",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $0<\\epsilon\\le1/10$ in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:residual-information\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:residual-information\">6</a>. For sufficiently large $d$ the following bounds hold.*\n\n\n1.  *If $G$ is Gaussian, $p\\ge3/5$, and $0\\le b\\le d/2$, the beta-variance bound (with the separate deterministic case at $b=0$) and the angular cap just described give $$\\label{eq:endpoint-gaussian}\n     I(S;V\\mid G,GS)\\ge\n     \\tfrac12(d-b-1)\\log\\frac1{5\\epsilon}-\\log2.$$*\n\n2.  *If $G$ is a Haar frame, $p\\ge2/3$, and $b=\\lfloor d/4\\rfloor$, Markov’s bound $\\mathbb P\\{R<1/2\\}\\le4b/(3d)\\le1/3$ gives $$\\label{eq:endpoint-haar}\n     I(S;V\\mid G,GS)\\ge\n     \\tfrac13(d-b-1)\\log\\frac1{4\\epsilon}-\\log2.$$*\n\n\n3.  *If $G$ is Gaussian, $p\\ge3/5$, and $b=\\lfloor d/2\\rfloor$, the direct Gaussian bound $\\mathbb P\\{R<1/2\\}\\le90/d$ gives $$\\label{eq:endpoint-bins}\n     I(S;V\\mid G,GS)\\ge\n     \\tfrac12(d-b-1)\\log\\frac1{4\\epsilon}-\\log2.$$*\n\n*Each right side is at least $c\\,d\\log(1/\\epsilon)$ for an absolute $c>0$ and all sufficiently large $d$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-borel-rules",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $d\\ge2$, a finite horizon $T$, and finite state sets. For fixed data-independent rule parameters, suppose each transition and stopping coordinate is measurable in the completion of the Borel sigma field on $\\mathbb R^d\\times\\mathbb R$ under $\\lambda(dx,dy)=\\gamma_{1,d}(dx)\\,dy$, where $\\gamma_{1,d}$ is standard Gaussian probability on $\\mathbb R^d$. The output law depends only on the terminal state and stopping index. There are Borel transition and stopping kernels, with the same state sets and output laws, having the same joint law of the signal, state path, stopping index, and output when $S\\sim\\sigma_d$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },

The output law depends only on the terminal state and stopping index.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-padding",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a learner in Definition <a href=\"#def:streaming-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:streaming-model\">3</a> under the uniform prior. If its average success is at least $p$, some fixed shared-seed conditional experiment has success at least $p$. Its initialization remains independent of the signal and samples, and its fixed-seed Borel replacement has the same success. In this Borel experiment the initialization and all remaining randomized rules can be represented by data-independent variables chosen before sampling, followed by deterministic state-based rules. For every $p'<p$, some realization of those variables gives deterministic rules with average success at least $p'$.*\n\n\n*Padding a learner stopped by $T$ so that its halted states are absorbing, while retaining the stopping index and postponing the output, uses at most $(T+2)2^M$ states at every layer. Hence every padded state has entropy at most $$\\label{eq:padded-entropy}\n h_T=M\\log2+\\log(T+2).$$ Extra ignored samples may be generated to complete a last block.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-padding",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a learner in Definition <a href=\"#def:streaming-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:streaming-model\">3</a> under the uniform prior.           "source_id": "PM-140.2"

        "exact_quote": "Let $t\\ge1$ and suppose $K<\\infty$. Let $W$ be finite-valued and suppose that every pair $(S_i,W)$ has the same law as $(S_1,W)$. In the conclusion write $S=S_1$. If $B$ is independent of $(S_1,\\ldots,S_t,W)$, then $$\\label{eq:replica-information}\n t I(S;W\\mid B,\\phi_B(S))\\le H(W)+K-K',\\qquad 0\\le K'\\le K.$$ The same statement holds in a conditional experiment when all of these hypotheses hold there, including the matching pair laws and the independence of $B$. Equivalently, the left side may use a separate representative of the common signal–message pair law if it is coupled independently with $B$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-projection-fibers",
        "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le k\\le d-2$ and let $P^0$ be either $\\sigma_d$ or standard Gaussian probability on $\\mathbb R^d$. Every full-row-rank $k\\times d$ matrix $A$ projects $P^0$ to a Lebesgue density $p_A^0$. With $A^\\dagger=A^{\\mathsf T}(AA^{\\mathsf T})^{-1}$, the spherical density is $$\\label{eq:spherical-projection-density}\n p_A^0(y)=\\frac{\\Gamma(d/2)}{\\pi^{k/2}\\Gamma((d-k)/2)}\n \\frac{(1-\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert^2)^{(d-k-2)/2}}\n      {\\sqrt{\\det(AA^{\\mathsf T})}}\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert<1\\}}.$$ For $\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert<1$, its conditional law $P^0_{A,y}$ is uniform on the sphere centered at $A^\\dagger y$, of radius $\\sqrt{1-\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert^2}$, in the affine space parallel to $\\ker A$. For the Gaussian base, $p_A^0$ is the density of $N(0,AA^{\\mathsf T})$, and the conditional point is $A^\\dagger y$ plus a standard Gaussian in $\\ker A$. These conditional laws have jointly measurable versions; unused fibers may be assigned any fixed probability law.*\n\n\n*If $m\\ge1$, $t=m+1$, and $k+m\\le d-2$, then $J>0$ almost surely under $(P^0)^{\\otimes t}$ and $$\\label{eq:inverse-affine-volume}\n \\int J(\\mathbf u)^{-k}\\,(P^0)^{\\otimes t}(d\\mathbf u)<\\infty.$$ If $k+m\\le d-3$, the same assertion holds with exponent $k+1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },

          "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le k\\le d-2$ and let $P^0$ be either $\\sigma_d$ or standard Gaussian probability on $\\mathbb R^d$. Every full-row-rank $k\\times d$ matrix $A$ projects $P^0$ to a Lebesgue density $p_A^0$. With $A^\\dagger=A^{\\mathsf T}(AA^{\\mathsf T})^{-1}$, the spherical density is $$\\label{eq:spherical-projection-density}\n p_A^0(y)=\\frac{\\Gamma(d/2)}{\\pi^{k/2}\\Gamma((d-k)/2)}\n \\frac{(1-\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert^2)^{(d-k-2)/2}}\n      {\\sqrt{\\det(AA^{\\mathsf T})}}\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert<1\\}}.$$ For $\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert<1$, its conditional law $P^0_{A,y}$ is uniform on the sphere centered at $A^\\dagger y$, of radius $\\sqrt{1-\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert^2}$, in the affine space parallel to $\\ker A$. For the Gaussian base, $p_A^0$ is the density of $N(0,AA^{\\mathsf T})$, and the conditional point is $A^\\dagger y$ plus a standard Gaussian in $\\ker A$. These conditional laws have jointly measurable versions; unused fibers may be assigned any fixed probability law.*\n\n\n*If $m\\ge1$, $t=m+1$, and $k+m\\le d-2$, then $J>0$ almost surely under $(P^0)^{\\otimes t}$ and $$\\label{eq:inverse-affine-volume}\n \\int J(\\mathbf u)^{-k}\\,(P^0)^{\\otimes t}(d\\mathbf u)<\\infty.$$ If $k+m\\le d-3$, the same assertion holds with exponent $k+1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"

      "exact_statement": "Let $1\\le k\\le d-2$ and let $P^0$ be either $\\sigma_d$ or standard Gaussian probability on $\\mathbb R^d$. With $A^\\dagger=A^{\\mathsf T}(AA^{\\mathsf T})^{-1}$, the spherical density is $$\\label{eq:spherical-projection-density}\n p_A^0(y)=\\frac{\\Gamma(d/2)}{\\pi^{k/2}\\Gamma((d-k)/2)}\n \\frac{(1-\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert^2)^{(d-k-2)/2}}\n      {\\sqrt{\\det(AA^{\\mathsf T})}}\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert<1\\}}.$$ For $\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert<1$, its conditional law $P^0_{A,y}$ is uniform on the sphere centered at $A^\\dagger y$, of radius $\\sqrt{1-\\lVert A^\\dagger y\\rVert^2}$, in the affine space parallel to $\\ker A$. For the Gaussian base, $p_A^0$ is the density of $N(0,AA^{\\mathsf T})$, and the conditional point is $A^\\dagger y$ plus a standard Gaussian in $\\ker A$. These conditional laws have jointly measurable versions; unused fibers may be assigned any fixed probability law.*\n\n\n*If $m\\ge1$, $t=m+1$, and $k+m\\le d-2$, then $J>0$ almost surely under $(P^0)^{\\otimes t}$ and $$\\label{eq:inverse-affine-volume}\n \\int J(\\mathbf u)^{-k}\\,(P^0)^{\\otimes t}(d\\mathbf u)<\\infty.$$ If $k+m\\le d-3$, the same assertion holds with exponent $k+1$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-equal-label-measure",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k,m\\ge1$, $t=m+1$, and $k+m\\le d-2$. Let $P^0$ be either uniform probability on $S^{d-1}$ or standard Gaussian probability on $\\mathbb R^d$, and let $\\nu=fP^0$ for a bounded nonnegative Borel function $f$. With the notation in <a href=\"#eq:equal-label-geometry\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:equal-label-geometry\">[eq:equal-label-geometry]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:finite-tilt\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-tilt\">[eq:finite-tilt]</a>, for every nonnegative Borel function $H(A,y,\\mathbf u)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\int\\gamma_{k,d}(dA)\\int_{\\mathbb R^k}p_A(y)^t\n       \\int H(A,y,\\mathbf u)\\,\\nu_{A,y}^{\\otimes t}(d\\mathbf u)\\,dy\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\quad=(2\\pi)^{-km/2}\\int J(\\mathbf u)^{-k}\\,\\nu^{\\otimes t}(d\\mathbf u)\n       \\int H(A,Au_1,\\mathbf u)\\,\\gamma_{k,d}^{F_{\\mathbf u}}(dA).\n \\label{eq:equal-label-measure}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Both sides are finite measures before the test $H$ is inserted. They are supported on $Au_i=y$ for every $i$, full-column-rank $D_{\\mathbf u}$, and full-row-rank $A$. In the spherical case the common fiber has positive radius. Thus <a href=\"#eq:equal-label-measure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:equal-label-measure\">[eq:equal-label-measure]</a> is an identity of finite measures on $(A,y,\\mathbf u)$; the right side places $y$ at $Au_1$.*\n\n\n*For a full-rank $A$, alternatively define $p_A(y)$ by the lower limit of its radius-$1/n$ Lebesgue ball averages or by the lower limit of its Gaussian convolutions with covariance $n^{-2}I_k$. Each choice is jointly measurable and agrees with <a href=\"#eq:finite-tilt\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-tilt\">[eq:finite-tilt]</a> for Lebesgue-almost every $y$. All three versions agree at $y=Au_1$, with a finite positive value, almost everywhere for the finite reference measure $$\\label{eq:equal-label-reference}\n R(d\\mathbf u,dA)=\\nu^{\\otimes t}(d\\mathbf u)\n                 \\gamma_{k,d}^{F_{\\mathbf u}}(dA).$$ When $\\nu=0$ the last assertion is vacuous. The equality is obtained using either ball or Gaussian approximate identities in both integration orders for the base law $P^0$, then multiplying the resulting measure identity by the bounded density factors.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },

They are supported on $Au_i=y$ for every $i$, full-column-rank $D_{\\mathbf u}$, and full-row-rank $A$. In the spherical case the common fiber has positive radius. The equality is obtained using either ball or Gaussian approximate identities in both integration orders for the base law $P^0$, then multiplying the resulting measure identity by the bounded density factors.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-replica-likelihood",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:equal-label-measure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:equal-label-measure\">15</a>, suppose $p=fP^0$ is a probability. Draw $A\\sim\\gamma_{k,d}$ independently of $S\\sim p$, put $Y=AS$, and conditionally on $(A,Y)$ draw $t$ independent replicas from $p_{A,Y}$. Let $Q$ be the law of their tuple together with $A$, and let $R=p^{\\otimes t}(d\\mathbf u)\\gamma_{k,d}^{F_{\\mathbf u}}(dA)$. Then $R$ is a probability and $$\\label{eq:replica-likelihood}\n \\frac{dQ}{dR}(\\mathbf u,A)\n =(2\\pi)^{-km/2}J(\\mathbf u)^{-k}p_A(Au_1)^{-m}.$$ In particular, the tuple marginal of $Q$ is absolutely continuous relative to $p^{\\otimes t}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-replica-likelihood",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:equal-label-measure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:equal-label-measure\">15</a>, suppose $p=fP^0$ is a probability. Then $R$ is a probability and $$\\label{eq:replica-likelihood}\n \\frac{dQ}{dR}(\\mathbf u,A)\n =(2\\pi)^{-km/2}J(\\mathbf u)^{-k}p_A(Au_1)^{-m}.$$ In particular, the tuple marginal of $Q$ is absolutely continuous relative to $p^{\\otimes t}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:equal-label-measure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:equal-label-measure\">15</a>, suppose $p=fP^0$ is a probability. Then $R$ is a probability and $$\\label{eq:replica-likelihood}\n \\frac{dQ}{dR}(\\mathbf u,A)\n =(2\\pi)^{-km/2}J(\\mathbf u)^{-k}p_A(Au_1)^{-m}.$$ In particular, the tuple marginal of $Q$ is absolutely continuous relative to $p^{\\otimes t}$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-replica-logs",
        "exact_quote": "In Corollary <a href=\"#cor:replica-likelihood\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cor:replica-likelihood\">16</a>, let $p=f\\sigma_d$ with $0\\le f\\le L<\\infty$. Under the replica law $Q$, both $\\log p_A(Y)$ and $\\log J(S_1,\\ldots,S_t)$ are absolutely integrable. Consequently the tuple total correlation is finite and satisfies $$\\label{eq:replica-correlation-upper}\n K\\le -\\frac{km}{2}\\log(2\\pi)-k\\mathbb E_Q\\log J-m\\mathbb E_Q\\log p_A(Y).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-test",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be an $\\ell\\times m$ standard Gaussian matrix and set $$Z_{k,m}=\\mathbb E\\det(G^{\\mathsf T}G)^{-k/2}.$$ This constant is finite and positive. The nonnegative function $$\\label{eq:gaussian-projected-test}\n \\Lambda(B,U_1,\\ldots,U_t)\n =\\frac{c}{Z_{k,m}}\n  \\det((BD)^{\\mathsf T}(BD))^{-k/2}\n  p_{C_0B}(C_0U_1)^{-m},$$ with value zero on the exceptional null sets, has expectation one under the law that draws $B$ and projects independent $p$-distributed signals. Conditional on the replica tuple and its original matrix $A$, $\\widetilde A$ has law $\\gamma_{k,d}^{F}$; this law depends only on the tuple. If $K'$ is the projected total correlation of the replicas, then $$\\label{eq:gaussian-correlation-loss}\n K-K'\\le Ckm+\n m\\mathbb E\\!\\left[\\log p_{\\widetilde A}(\\widetilde A S_1)-\\log p_A(Y)\\right].$$ Every logarithm in this display is absolutely integrable.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-test",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be an $\\ell\\times m$ standard Gaussian matrix and set $$Z_{k,m}=\\mathbb E\\det(G^{\\mathsf T}G)^{-k/2}.$$ This constant is finite and positive. The nonnegative function $$\\label{eq:gaussian-projected-test}\n \\Lambda(B,U_1,\\ldots,U_t)\n =\\frac{c}{Z_{k,m}}\n  \\det((BD)^{\\mathsf T}(BD))^{-k/2}\n  p_{C_0B}(C_0U_1)^{-m},$$ with value zero on the exceptional null sets, has expectation one under the law that draws $B$ and projects independent $p$-distributed signals. Conditional on the replica tuple and its original matrix $A$, $\\widetilde A$ has law $\\gamma_{k,d}^{F}$; this law depends only on the tuple. If $K'$ is the projected total correlation of the replicas, then $$\\label{eq:gaussian-correlation-loss}\n K-K'\\le Ckm+\n m\\mathbb E\\!\\left[\\log p_{\\widetilde A}(\\widetilde A S_1)-\\log p_A(Y)\\right].$$ Every logarithm in this display is absolutely integrable.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be an $\\ell\\times m$ standard Gaussian matrix and set $$Z_{k,m}=\\mathbb E\\det(G^{\\mathsf T}G)^{-k/2}.$$ This constant is finite and positive.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-distance-block",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P=f\\sigma_d$ be a probability with a nonnegative Borel density $f\\le e^b$, where $b\\ge0$. Let $S\\sim P$, let $A$ be an independent $k\\times d$ standard Gaussian matrix, and put $Y=AS$. Let a finite message $W$ be drawn from a kernel of $(A,Y)$, conditionally independently of $S$. Let $B$ be a $p\\times d$ standard Gaussian matrix independent of the entire joint experiment producing $(S,A,Y,W)$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-haar-block",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma$, let $V$ be a finite-valued state, and let a block consist of $k=\\lfloor d/16\\rfloor$ fresh independent standard Gaussian designs, independent of $(S,V)$ and the past, with their exact labels. Suppose a finite-valued state $W$ is generated by a measurable randomized kernel from $V$ and the full block, with no other retained information from the past. Let $B$ be a Haar $\\ell=\\lfloor d/4\\rfloor$ frame drawn independently of the entire joint experiment producing $S,V$, the block, and $W$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n I(S;W\\mid V,B,BS)\\le{}&\\frac{H(W\\mid V)}{m}+Cd\n       +\\log(1+Cd+H(V))\\notag\\\\\n &+C\\frac{H(V)+\\log d}{d^2},\n \\qquad m=\\lfloor d/16\\rfloor+1.\n \\label{haar:state-budget}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constant $C$ is absolute. If every state has logarithmic width at most $H_*=o(d^2)$, the left side is at most $Cd$ for all sufficiently large $d$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-haar-block",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma$, let $V$ be a finite-valued state, and let a block consist of $k=\\lfloor d/16\\rfloor$ fresh independent standard Gaussian designs, independent of $(S,V)$ and the past, with their exact labels. Suppose a finite-valued state $W$ is generated by a measurable randomized kernel from $V$ and the full block, with no other retained information from the past. Let $B$ be a Haar $\\ell=\\lfloor d/4\\rfloor$ frame drawn independently of the entire joint experiment producing $S,V$, the block, and $W$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n I(S;W\\mid V,B,BS)\\le{}&\\frac{H(W\\mid V)}{m}+Cd\n       +\\log(1+Cd+H(V))\\notag\\\\\n &+C\\frac{H(V)+\\log d}{d^2},\n \\qquad m=\\lfloor d/16\\rfloor+1.\n \\label{haar:state-budget}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constant $C$ is absolute. If every state has logarithmic width at most $H_*=o(d^2)$, the left side is at most $Cd$ for all sufficiently large $d$.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma$, let $V$ be a finite-valued state, and let a block consist of $k=\\lfloor d/16\\rfloor$ fresh independent standard Gaussian designs, independent of $(S,V)$ and the past, with their exact labels.       "explanation": "Cancelling most of the height terms gives a block information bound that is linear in the dimension when state entropies are subquadratic.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-haar-terminal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma$, let $V_f$ be finite-valued, and let the unit-valued output $\\widehat S$ be generated from $V_f$ by a measurable kernel using fresh randomness, conditionally independently of $S$. Let $B$ be a Haar $\\ell=\\lfloor d/4\\rfloor$ frame independent of $(S,V_f,\\widehat S)$. If $\\mathbb P\\{\\arccos\\langle\\widehat S,S\\rangle\\le\\epsilon\\}\\ge2/3$ for $0<\\epsilon\\le1/10$, then $$\\label{haar:terminal-information}\n I(S;V_f\\mid B,BS)\n \\ge\\frac{d-\\ell-1}{3}\\log\\frac1{4\\epsilon}-\\log2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-haar-terminal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma$, let $V_f$ be finite-valued, and let the unit-valued output $\\widehat S$ be generated from $V_f$ by a measurable kernel using fresh randomness, conditionally independently of $S$. Let $B$ be a Haar $\\ell=\\lfloor d/4\\rfloor$ frame independent of $(S,V_f,\\widehat S)$. If $\\mathbb P\\{\\arccos\\langle\\widehat S,S\\rangle\\le\\epsilon\\}\\ge2/3$ for $0<\\epsilon\\le1/10$, then $$\\label{haar:terminal-information}\n I(S;V_f\\mid B,BS)\n \\ge\\frac{d-\\ell-1}{3}\\log\\frac1{4\\epsilon}-\\log2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma$, let $V_f$ be finite-valued, and let the unit-valued output $\\widehat S$ be generated from $V_f$ by a measurable kernel using fresh randomness, conditionally independently of $S$. If $\\mathbb P\\{\\arccos\\langle\\widehat S,S\\rangle\\le\\epsilon\\}\\ge2/3$ for $0<\\epsilon\\le1/10$, then $$\\label{haar:terminal-information}\n I(S;V_f\\mid B,BS)\n \\ge\\frac{d-\\ell-1}{3}\\log\\frac1{4\\epsilon}-\\log2.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-haar-streaming",
        "exact_quote": "For each dimension $d$, let $S\\sim\\sigma$ and consider a learner in Definition <a href=\"#def:streaming-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:streaming-model\">3</a>, with deterministic horizon $T=T(d)$ and nonnegative integer memory $M=M(d)$. If $M(d)=o(d^2)$, $0<\\epsilon(d)\\le1/10$, and its angular success probability averaged over the signal, samples, and learner randomness is at least $2/3$, then $$T(d)=\\Omega\\bigl(d\\log(1/\\epsilon(d))\\bigr).$$ The implicit constant is absolute; the dimension threshold may depend on the memory sequence.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-haar-streaming",
          "exact_quote": "For each dimension $d$, let $S\\sim\\sigma$ and consider a learner in Definition <a href=\"#def:streaming-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:streaming-model\">3</a>, with deterministic horizon $T=T(d)$ and nonnegative integer memory $M=M(d)$.           "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For each dimension $d$, let $S\\sim\\sigma$ and consider a learner in Definition <a href=\"#def:streaming-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:streaming-model\">3</a>, with deterministic horizon $T=T(d)$ and nonnegative integer memory $M=M(d)$. If $M(d)=o(d^2)$, $0<\\epsilon(d)\\le1/10$, and its angular success probability averaged over the signal, samples, and learner randomness is at least $2/3$, then $$T(d)=\\Omega\\bigl(d\\log(1/\\epsilon(d))\\bigr).$$ The implicit constant is absolute; the dimension threshold may depend on the memory sequence.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-syn-block",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P=f\\sigma_d$ be a probability, where $f$ is a nonnegative Borel function satisfying $f\\le\\Lambda<\\infty$, with $\\Lambda\\ge1$. Draw $X\\sim P$ and $A\\sim\\gamma_{k,d}$ independently. Let $z=z(A,AX)$ be a measurable function taking at most $K\\ge1$ values. Independently of this entire experiment draw $B\\sim\\gamma_{m,d}$, and put $Y=BX$. With the parameters in <a href=\"#syn:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"syn:parameters\">[syn:parameters]</a>, $$\\label{syn:block-bound}\n I(X;z\\mid B,Y)\n \\le \\frac{\\log K}{\\ell}\n       +C\\bigl[d+\\log(1+\\log\\Lambda)\\bigr],$$ where $C$ is absolute and independent of $P,\\Lambda,K$, and the message rule.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-syn-block",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P=f\\sigma_d$ be a probability, where $f$ is a nonnegative Borel function satisfying $f\\le\\Lambda<\\infty$, with $\\Lambda\\ge1$. Independently of this entire experiment draw $B\\sim\\gamma_{m,d}$, and put $Y=BX$. With the parameters in <a href=\"#syn:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"syn:parameters\">[syn:parameters]</a>, $$\\label{syn:block-bound}\n I(X;z\\mid B,Y)\n \\le \\frac{\\log K}{\\ell}\n       +C\\bigl[d+\\log(1+\\log\\Lambda)\\bigr],$$ where $C$ is absolute and independent of $P,\\Lambda,K$, and the message rule.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"

        "exact_quote": "Let $P=f\\sigma_d$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#syn:block\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"syn:block\">32</a>. Fix a tuple $\\mathbf x\\in(S^{d-1})^\\ell$ of affine rank $u$, and put $$x=x_1,\\qquad L=L_{\\mathbf x},\\qquad\n \\delta(s)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(s-x,L).$$ Draw $B\\sim\\gamma_{m,d}$. Given $B$, draw $F\\sim\\gamma_{k,m}^{BL}$, so each row of $F$ is a standard Gaussian in $(BL)^\\perp$. Then $$\\label{syn:synthetic-bound}\n \\mathbb E_{B,F}g_{FB}(FBx)\n \\le (2\\pi)^{-k/2}a_q\\int\\delta(s)^{-k}\\,P(ds),\\qquad\n a_q=\\mathbb E\\det(HH^{\\mathsf T})^{-1/2}\\le(q-k-1)^{-k/2},$$ where $H$ is a standard Gaussian $k\\times q$ matrix.*\n\n\n\n\n\n\n*There is an absolute $C_0>0$ such that the following holds with $$\\label{syn:cutoff}\n r_*=\\exp\\!\\left[-\\frac{C_0(d+\\log\\Lambda)}{d-1-u-k}\\right]\\in(0,1).$$ For every full-row-rank $k\\times d$ matrix $A$, put $a=\\lVert A\\rVert_{\\mathrm{op}}$ and define $$\\begin{aligned}\n U_A(b)={}&(2\\pi)^{-k/2}\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\lVert b\\rVert\\le a\\}}\n \\left[1+a^k\\int g_A(v)\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\lVert v-b\\rVert\\le2a\\}}\\right. \\notag\\\\[-2mm]\n &\\hspace{42mm}\\left.\n       {}\\cdot\\min\\{(ar_*)^{-k},\\lVert v-b\\rVert^{-k}\\}\\,dv\\right].\n \\label{syn:majorant}\n\\end{aligned}$$ At $v=b$, the second entry in the minimum is interpreted as $+\\infty$. If $Ax=b$ and $L\\subset\\ker A$, then $$\\label{syn:kernel-bound}\n (2\\pi)^{-k/2}\\int\\delta(s)^{-k}\\,P(ds)\\le U_A(b).$$ Writing $V_k$ for the volume of the Euclidean unit ball in $\\mathbb R^k$, the same function satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int U_A(b)\\,db\n &\\le (2\\pi)^{-k/2}V_k a^k[2+k\\log(2/r_*)],\n \\label{syn:majorant-mass}\\\\\n \\mathbb E_{A\\sim\\gamma_{k,d}}\\log\\int U_A(b)\\,db\n &\\le C\\bigl[d+\\log(1+\\log\\Lambda)\\bigr].\n \\label{syn:majorant-log}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The logarithm in the last display is absolutely integrable, and $\\mathbb E_A\\log(1+\\int U_A)<\\infty$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-syn-logs",
        "exact_quote": "In the replica experiment $Q$, enlarged by the independent $B$ and the conditional $F$ above, $$\\mathbb E|\\log w(\\mathbf Y)|+\\mathbb E|\\log\\Xi|\n       +\\mathbb E|\\log R_B(\\mathbf Y)|<\\infty.$$ If $G$ is a standard Gaussian $m\\times u$ matrix, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E\\log w(\\mathbf Y)\n &=\\mathbb E\\log w(\\mathbf X)-\\frac{k}{2}\\mathbb E\\log\\det(G^{\\mathsf T}G),\n \\label{syn:gram-log}\\\\\n \\frac12\\mathbb E\\log\\det(G^{\\mathsf T}G)&\\le\\frac u2\\log m.\n \\label{syn:gram-upper}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $$\\label{syn:correlation-lower}\n \\mathcal C_Y\n \\ge\\mathbb E\\log R_B(\\mathbf Y)\n \\ge\\mathbb E\\log w(\\mathbf Y)-u\\,\\mathbb E\\log\\Xi.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-block",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu=f\\sigma$ be a probability measure with a Borel density $0\\le f\\le F<\\infty$, where $F\\ge1$. Draw $S\\sim\\mu$ and standard Gaussian matrices $A\\in\\mathbb R^{n\\times d}$ and $B\\in\\mathbb R^{p\\times d}$, all independently. For an integer $w\\ge1$, a message $J$ takes values in a fixed $w$-point alphabet (unused symbols are allowed) and is drawn by an arbitrary measurable probability kernel from $(A,AS)$; conditional on $(A,AS)$, its randomness is independent of $(S,B)$. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\\label{inc:block-bound}\n I_\\mu(S;J\\mid B,BS)\n \\le \\frac{\\log w}{k}+Cd+C\\log(1+\\log F).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\\label{inc:block-bound}\n I_\\mu(S;J\\mid B,BS)\n \\le \\frac{\\log w}{k}+Cd+C\\log(1+\\log F).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"

      "conclusion": {

        "exact_quote": "Put $Z_i=Bs_i$. After $A,C$ are discarded under $R$, $$\\label{inc:product-law}\n \\mathcal L_R(\\mathbf s\\mid B,Z_1,\\ldots,Z_k,J)\n =\\bigotimes_{i=1}^k\\mu_{B,Z_i}.$$ Consequently, $$\\label{inc:entropy-lower}\n \\operatorname{KL}(Q\\|R)\\ge kI_\\mu(S;J\\mid B,BS).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-inc-product",
          "exact_quote": "Put $Z_i=Bs_i$. After $A,C$ are discarded under $R$, $$\\label{inc:product-law}\n \\mathcal L_R(\\mathbf s\\mid B,Z_1,\\ldots,Z_k,J)\n =\\bigotimes_{i=1}^k\\mu_{B,Z_i}.$$ Consequently, $$\\label{inc:entropy-lower}\n \\operatorname{KL}(Q\\|R)\\ge kI_\\mu(S;J\\mid B,BS).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Put $Z_i=Bs_i$. After $A,C$ are discarded under $R$, $$\\label{inc:product-law}\n \\mathcal L_R(\\mathbf s\\mid B,Z_1,\\ldots,Z_k,J)\n =\\bigotimes_{i=1}^k\\mu_{B,Z_i}.$$ Consequently, $$\\label{inc:entropy-lower}\n \\operatorname{KL}(Q\\|R)\\ge kI_\\mu(S;J\\mid B,BS).$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-projection",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $L\\in\\mathbb R^{q\\times d}$ has full row rank and $1\\le q\\le d-2$. In the interior $$E_L=\\{t:t^{\\mathsf T}(LL^{\\mathsf T})^{-1}t<1\\}$$ define a finite measure on $\\{s\\in\\mathbb S^{d-1}:Ls=t\\}$ by $$\\label{inc:fiber-measure}\n \\lambda_{L,t}(ds)=\n \\frac{f(s)}{|\\mathbb S^{d-1}|\\,j_L(s)}\n \\,d\\operatorname{vol}_{d-1-q}(s),\\qquad\n j_L(s)=\\det\\!\\bigl(L(I-ss^{\\mathsf T})L^{\\mathsf T}\\bigr)^{1/2}.$$ Set $\\lambda_{L,t}=0$ outside $E_L$. Its mass and normalization are exactly the versions recalled in <a href=\"#inc:recalled-fibers\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"inc:recalled-fibers\">[inc:recalled-fibers]</a>: $$p_L(t)=\\lambda_{L,t}(\\mathbb S^{d-1}),\\qquad\n \\mu_{L,t}=\\lambda_{L,t}/p_L(t)\\quad\\text{where }p_L(t)>0.$$ The finite-measure kernel is jointly measurable in $(L,t)$. Moreover, $$\\label{inc:ellipsoid-bound}\n V_L\\|p_L\\|_\\infty\\le Fd^d,\\qquad\n V_L=|E_L|=v_q\\det(LL^{\\mathsf T})^{1/2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-inc-projection",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $L\\in\\mathbb R^{q\\times d}$ has full row rank and $1\\le q\\le d-2$. Its mass and normalization are exactly the versions recalled in <a href=\"#inc:recalled-fibers\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"inc:recalled-fibers\">[inc:recalled-fibers]</a>: $$p_L(t)=\\lambda_{L,t}(\\mathbb S^{d-1}),\\qquad\n \\mu_{L,t}=\\lambda_{L,t}/p_L(t)\\quad\\text{where }p_L(t)>0.$$ The finite-measure kernel is jointly measurable in $(L,t)$. Moreover, $$\\label{inc:ellipsoid-bound}\n V_L\\|p_L\\|_\\infty\\le Fd^d,\\qquad\n V_L=|E_L|=v_q\\det(LL^{\\mathsf T})^{1/2}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $L\\in\\mathbb R^{q\\times d}$ has full row rank and $1\\le q\\le d-2$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-swap",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X,Z$ be second-countable Riemannian manifolds, and let $G:X\\times Z\\to\\mathbb R^a$ be smooth. On a relatively open subset $\\Omega$ of $G^{-1}(0)$, suppose both partial differentials $d_xG$ and $d_zG$ are onto. Write $J_xG,J_zG$ for their normal Jacobians. For every nonnegative measurable $h$ on $\\Omega$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\int_Z\\int_{\\{x:(x,z)\\in\\Omega\\}}\n       \\frac{h(x,z)}{J_xG(x,z)}\n       \\,d\\operatorname{vol}_{\\dim X-a}(x)\\,d\\operatorname{vol}_Z(z)\n       \\notag\\\\\n &\\qquad=\n \\int_X\\int_{\\{z:(x,z)\\in\\Omega\\}}\n       \\frac{h(x,z)}{J_zG(x,z)}\n       \\,d\\operatorname{vol}_{\\dim Z-a}(z)\\,d\\operatorname{vol}_X(x).\n \\label{inc:swap-identity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The fiber volumes are induced volumes, and the equality holds for nonnegative integrals even when their common value is infinite.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-inc-swap",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X,Z$ be second-countable Riemannian manifolds, and let $G:X\\times Z\\to\\mathbb R^a$ be smooth. On a relatively open subset $\\Omega$ of $G^{-1}(0)$, suppose both partial differentials $d_xG$ and $d_zG$ are onto. Write $J_xG,J_zG$ for their normal Jacobians. For every nonnegative measurable $h$ on $\\Omega$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\int_Z\\int_{\\{x:(x,z)\\in\\Omega\\}}\n       \\frac{h(x,z)}{J_xG(x,z)}\n       \\,d\\operatorname{vol}_{\\dim X-a}(x)\\,d\\operatorname{vol}_Z(z)\n       \\notag\\\\\n &\\qquad=\n \\int_X\\int_{\\{z:(x,z)\\in\\Omega\\}}\n       \\frac{h(x,z)}{J_zG(x,z)}\n       \\,d\\operatorname{vol}_{\\dim Z-a}(z)\\,d\\operatorname{vol}_X(x).\n \\label{inc:swap-identity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The fiber volumes are induced volumes, and the equality holds for nonnegative integrals even when their common value is infinite.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $X,Z$ be second-countable Riemannian manifolds, and let $G:X\\times Z\\to\\mathbb R^a$ be smooth. On a relatively open subset $\\Omega$ of $G^{-1}(0)$, suppose both partial differentials $d_xG$ and $d_zG$ are onto. Write $J_xG,J_zG$ for their normal Jacobians. For every nonnegative measurable $h$ on $\\Omega$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\int_Z\\int_{\\{x:(x,z)\\in\\Omega\\}}\n       \\frac{h(x,z)}{J_xG(x,z)}\n       \\,d\\operatorname{vol}_{\\dim X-a}(x)\\,d\\operatorname{vol}_Z(z)\n       \\notag\\\\\n &\\qquad=\n \\int_X\\int_{\\{z:(x,z)\\in\\Omega\\}}\n       \\frac{h(x,z)}{J_zG(x,z)}\n       \\,d\\operatorname{vol}_{\\dim Z-a}(z)\\,d\\operatorname{vol}_X(x).\n \\label{inc:swap-identity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The fiber volumes are induced volumes, and the equality holds for nonnegative integrals even when their common value is infinite.",
      "explanation": "Allows interchange of integration order on a regular incidence set using normal Jacobians.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-inc-swap",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X,Z$ be second-countable Riemannian manifolds",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-incidence-swap",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-inc-swap",
          "exact_quote": "For every nonnegative measurable $h$ on $\\Omega$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.2"
        }
